+24|  function indexToColLetter(index) {
+25|    let col = '';
+26|    let temp = index;
+27|    while (temp > 0) {
+28|      let rem = (temp - 1) % 26;
+29|      col = String.fromCharCode(65 + rem) + col;
+30|      temp = Math.floor((temp - 1) / 26);
+31|    }
+32|    return col;
+33|  }
+34|
+35|  // Parse cell reference: "A1", "$B$2", "C$3", "$D4" -> { colStr, rowNum, 
colIndex, isColAbs, isRowAbs }
+36|  function parseCellRef(ref) {
+37|    const match = ref.match(/^(\$?)([A-Za-z]+)(\$?)([1-9][0-9]*)$/);
+38|    if (!match) return null;
+39|    const isColAbs = match[1] === '$';
+40|    const colStr = match[2].toUpperCase();
+41|    const isRowAbs = match[3] === '$';
+42|    const rowNum = parseInt(match[4], 10);
+43|    const colIndex = colLetterToIndex(colStr);
+44|    return {
+45|      raw: ref,
+46|      colStr,
+47|      rowNum,
+48|      colIndex,
+49|      isColAbs,
+50|      isRowAbs,
+51|      normalized: `${colStr}${rowNum}`
+52|    };
+53|  }
+54|
+55|  // Expand range: "A1:B3" -> ["A1", "A2", "A3", "B1", "B2", "B3"]
+56|  function expandRange(startRef, endRef) {
+57|    const pStart = parseCellRef(startRef);
+58|    const pEnd = parseCellRef(endRef);
+59|    if (!pStart || !pEnd) return [];
+60|
+61|    const minCol = Math.min(pStart.colIndex, pEnd.colIndex);
+62|    const maxCol = Math.max(pStart.colIndex, pEnd.colIndex);
+63|    const minRow = Math.min(pStart.rowNum, pEnd.rowNum);
+64|    const maxRow = Math.max(pStart.rowNum, pEnd.rowNum);
+65|
+66|    const cells = [];
+67|    for (let c = minCol; c <= maxCol; c++) {
+68|      const colLetter = indexToColLetter(c);
+69|      for (let r = minRow; r <= maxRow; r++) {
+70|        cells.push(`${colLetter}${r}`);
+71|      }
+72|    }
+73|    return cells;
+74|  }
+75|
+76|  // Shift cell reference by deltaRow and deltaCol (respecting $ absolute 
modifiers)
+77|  function shiftCellRef(ref, deltaRow, deltaCol) {
+78|    const p = parseCellRef(ref);
+79|    if (!p) return ref;
+80|
+81|    let targetCol = p.colIndex;
+82|    if (!p.isColAbs) {
+83|      targetCol += deltaCol;
+84|    }
+85|    let targetRow = p.rowNum;
+86|    if (!p.isRowAbs) {
+87|      targetRow += deltaRow;
+88|    }
+89|
+90|    if (targetCol < 1 || targetRow < 1) {
+91|      return '#REF!';
+92|    }
+93|
+94|    const colStr = indexToColLetter(targetCol);
+95|    const prefixCol = p.isColAbs ? '$' : '';
+96|    const prefixRow = p.isRowAbs ? '$' : '';
+97|    return `${prefixCol}${colStr}${prefixRow}${targetRow}`;
+98|  }
+99|
+100|  // Shift formula references
+101|  function shiftFormula(formulaStr, deltaRow, deltaCol) {
+102|    if (!formulaStr || !formulaStr.startsWith('=')) return formulaStr;
+103|    const formulaBody = formulaStr.slice(1);
+104|
+105|    // Replace range refs and cell refs using regex
+106|    // Range regex: 
(\$?[A-Za-z]+\$?[1-9][0-9]*):(\$?[A-Za-z]+\$?[1-9][0-9]*)
+107|    // Cell regex: (\$?[A-Za-z]+\$?[1-9][0-9]*)
+108|    const rangeRegex = 
/(\$?[A-Za-z]+\$?[1-9][0-9]*):(\$?[A-Za-z]+\$?[1-9][0-9]*)/g;
+109|    const cellRegex = 
/(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_:\$])(\$?[A-Za-z]+\$?[1-9][0-9]*)(?![A-Za-z0-9_:\(])/g;
+110|
+111|    let shifted = formulaBody.replace(rangeRegex, (match, ref1, ref2) => {
+112|      const s1 = shiftCellRef(ref1, deltaRow, deltaCol);
+113|      const s2 = shiftCellRef(ref2, deltaRow, deltaCol);
+114|      if (s1 === '#REF!' || s2 === '#REF!') return '#REF!';
+115|      return `${s1}:${s2}`;
+116|    });
+117|
+118|    shifted = shifted.replace(cellRegex, (match, prefix, ref) => {
+119|      const shiftedRef = shiftCellRef(ref, deltaRow, deltaCol);
+120|      return `${prefix}${shiftedRef}`;
+121|    });
+122|
+123|    return `=${shifted}`;
+124|  }
+125|
+126|  // Extract all referenced cell addresses from a formula string (returns 
array of uppercase normalized cell keys)
+127|  function extractDependencies(formulaStr) {
+128|    if (!formulaStr || !formulaStr.startsWith('=')) return [];
+129|    const deps = new Set();
+130|    const str = formulaStr.slice(1);
+131|
+132|    // Match ranges: A1:B10
+133|    const rangeRegex = 
/([A-Za-z]+\$?[1-9][0-9]*):([A-Za-z]+\$?[1-9][0-9]*)/g;
+134|    let match;
+135|    const stripped = str.replace(rangeRegex, (m, r1, r2) => {
+136|      const cells = expandRange(r1, r2);
+137|      cells.forEach(c => deps.add(c));
+138|      return '___RANGE___';
+139|    });
+140|
+141|    // Match single cell refs: A1, B2 (avoid matching function names 
followed by paren)
+142|    const cellRegex = /\b([A-Za-z]+[1-9][0-9]*)\b(?!\s*\()/g;
+143|    while ((match = cellRegex.exec(stripped)) !== null) {
+144|      const ref = match[1].toUpperCase();
+145|      // Ensure it's a valid cell ref
+146|      if (parseCellRef(ref)) {
+147|        deps.add(ref);
+148|      }
+149|    }
+150|
+151|    return Array.from(deps);
+152|  }
+153|
+154|  // --- Lexer / Tokenizer ---
+155|  const TOKEN_TYPES = {
+156|    NUMBER: 'NUMBER',
+157|    STRING: 'STRING',
+158|    IDENTIFIER: 'IDENTIFIER',
+159|    CELL_REF: 'CELL_REF',
+160|    RANGE_REF: 'RANGE_REF',
+161|    OPERATOR: 'OPERATOR',
+162|    LPAREN: 'LPAREN',
+163|    RPAREN: 'RPAREN',
+164|    COMMA: 'COMMA',
+165|    COLON: 'COLON'
+166|  };
+167|
+168|  function tokenize(input) {
+169|    const tokens = [];
+170|    let i = 0;
+171|    const str = input.startsWith('=') ? input.slice(1) : input;
+172|
+173|    while (i < str.length) {
+174|      const char = str[i];
+175|
+176|      if (/\s/.test(char)) {
+177|        i++;
+178|        continue;
+179|      }
+180|
+181|      if (char === '(') {
+182|        tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.LPAREN, value: '(' });
+183|        i++;
+184|        continue;
+185|      }
+186|      if (char === ')') {
+187|        tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.RPAREN, value: ')' });
+188|        i++;
+189|        continue;
+190|      }
+191|      if (char === ',') {
+192|        tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.COMMA, value: ',' });
+193|        i++;
+194|        continue;
+195|      }
+196|      if (char === ':') {
+197|        tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.COLON, value: ':' });
+198|        i++;
+199|        continue;
+200|      }
+201|
+202|      // Comparison / Multi-char operators
+203|      if (char === '<') {
+204|        if (str[i + 1] === '>') {
+205|          tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR, value: '<>' });
+206|          i += 2;
+207|          continue;
+208|        }
+209|        if (str[i + 1] === '=') {
+210|          tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR, value: '<=' });
+211|          i += 2;
+212|          continue;
+213|        }
+214|        tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR, value: '<' });
+215|        i++;
+216|        continue;
+217|      }
+218|      if (char === '>') {
+219|        if (str[i + 1] === '=') {
+220|          tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR, value: '>=' });
+221|          i += 2;
+222|          continue;
+223|        }
+224|        tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR, value: '>' });
+225|        i++;
+226|        continue;
+227|      }
+228|      if (char === '!' && str[i + 1] === '=') {
+229|        tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR, value: '!=' });
+230|        i += 2;
+231|        continue;
+232|      }
+233|      if (char === '=' || char === '+' || char === '-' || char === '*' || 
char === '/' || char === '^' || char === '%') {
+234|        tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR, value: char });
+235|        i++;
+236|        continue;
+237|      }
+238|
+239|      // Strings in quotes
+240|      if (char === '"' || char === "'") {
+241|        const quote = char;
+242|        let s = '';
+243|        i++;
+244|        while (i < str.length && str[i] !== quote) {
+245|          s += str[i];
+246|          i++;
+247|        }
+248|        if (i < str.length && str[i] === quote) {
+249|          i++;
+250|        }
+251|        tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.STRING, value: s });
+252|        continue;
+253|      }
+254|
+255|      // Numbers
+256|      if (/[0-9]/.test(char) || (char === '.' && /[0-9]/.test(str[i + 1])))
{
+257|        let numStr = '';
+258|        while (i < str.length && /[0-9.]/.test(str[i])) {
+259|          numStr += str[i];
+260|          i++;
+261|        }
+262|        tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.NUMBER, value: parseFloat(numStr) 
});
+263|        continue;
+264|      }
+265|
+266|      // Identifiers, Cell references, Ranges, or Function names
+267|      if (/[A-Za-z_\$]/.test(char)) {
+268|        let ident = '';
+269|        while (i < str.length && /[A-Za-z0-9_\$]/.test(str[i])) {
+270|          ident += str[i];
+271|          i++;
+272|        }
+273|
+274|        // Check if followed by colon for range: e.g. A1:B10
+275|        if (str[i] === ':') {
+276|          let j = i + 1;
+277|          while (j < str.length && /\s/.test(str[j])) j++;
+278|          if (/[A-Za-z0-9_\$]/.test(str[j])) {
+279|            let ident2 = '';
+280|            while (j < str.length && /[A-Za-z0-9_\$]/.test(str[j])) {
+281|              ident2 += str[j];
+282|              j++;
+283|            }
+284|            if (parseCellRef(ident) && parseCellRef(ident2)) {
+285|              tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.RANGE_REF, value: 
`${ident}:${ident2}` });
+286|              i = j;
+287|              continue;
+288|            }
+289|          }
+290|        }
+291|
+292|        // Check if it's a cell reference
+293|        if (parseCellRef(ident)) {
+294|          tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.CELL_REF, value: 
ident.toUpperCase() });
+295|          continue;
+296|        }
+297|
+298|        // Otherwise identifier (function name like SUM, AVG, MIN, MAX, 
COUNT, or TRUE/FALSE)
+299|        const upper = ident.toUpperCase();
+300|        if (upper === 'TRUE') {
+301|          tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.NUMBER, value: 1 });
+302|        } else if (upper === 'FALSE') {
+303|          tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.NUMBER, value: 0 });
+304|        } else {
+305|          tokens.push({ type: TOKEN_TYPES.IDENTIFIER, value: upper });
+306|        }
+307|        continue;
+308|      }
+309|
+310|      // Unknown character
+311|      throw new Error(`Unexpected character: '${char}'`);
+312|    }
+313|
+314|    return tokens;
+315|  }
+316|
+317|  // --- Parser (Recursive Descent) ---
+318|  class FormulaParser {
+319|    constructor(tokens, cellLookupFn) {
+320|      this.tokens = tokens;
+321|      this.pos = 0;
+322|      this.cellLookupFn = cellLookupFn || (() => 0);
+323|    }
+324|
+325|    peek() {
+326|      return this.tokens[this.pos] || null;
+327|    }
+328|
+329|    consume(expectedType, expectedValue) {
+330|      const token = this.peek();
+331|      if (!token) throw new Error('#SYNTAX!');
+332|      if (expectedType && token.type !== expectedType) {
+333|        throw new Error('#SYNTAX!');
+334|      }
+335|      if (expectedValue && token.value !== expectedValue) {
+336|        throw new Error('#SYNTAX!');
+337|      }
+338|      this.pos++;
+339|      return token;
+340|    }
+341|
+342|    parse() {
+343|      if (this.tokens.length === 0) return 0;
+344|      const result = this.parseExpression();
+345|      if (this.pos < this.tokens.length) {
+346|        throw new Error('#SYNTAX!');
+347|      }
+348|      return result;
+349|    }
+350|
+351|    // Comparison: =, <>, !=, <, <=, >, >=
+352|    parseExpression() {
+353|      let left = this.parseAdditive();
+354|
+355|      while (this.peek() && this.peek().type === TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR &&
+356|        ['=', '<>', '!=', '<', '<=', '>', 
'>='].includes(this.peek().value)) {
+357|        const op = this.consume().value;
+358|        const right = this.parseAdditive();
+359|        if (op === '=') left = (left === right) ? 1 : 0;
+360|        else if (op === '<>' || op === '!=') left = (left !== right) ? 1 : 
0;
+361|        else if (op === '<') left = (left < right) ? 1 : 0;
+362|        else if (op === '<=') left = (left <= right) ? 1 : 0;
+363|        else if (op === '>') left = (left > right) ? 1 : 0;
+364|        else if (op === '>=') left = (left >= right) ? 1 : 0;
+365|      }
+366|
+367|      return left;
+368|    }
+369|
+370|    // Addition, Subtraction: +, -
+371|    parseAdditive() {
+372|      let left = this.parseMultiplicative();
+373|
+374|      while (this.peek() && this.peek().type === TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR &&
+375|        (this.peek().value === '+' || this.peek().value === '-')) {
+376|        const op = this.consume().value;
+377|        const right = this.parseMultiplicative();
+378|        const lNum = this.toNumber(left);
+379|        const rNum = this.toNumber(right);
+380|        if (op === '+') left = lNum + rNum;
+381|        else if (op === '-') left = lNum - rNum;
+382|      }
+383|
+384|      return left;
+385|    }
+386|
+387|    // Multiplication, Division: *, /
+388|    parseMultiplicative() {
+389|      let left = this.parseExponentiation();
+390|
+391|      while (this.peek() && this.peek().type === TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR &&
+392|        (this.peek().value === '*' || this.peek().value === '/')) {
+393|        const op = this.consume().value;
+394|        const right = this.parseExponentiation();
+395|        const lNum = this.toNumber(left);
+396|        const rNum = this.toNumber(right);
+397|        if (op === '*') left = lNum * rNum;
+398|        else if (op === '/') {
+399|          if (rNum === 0) throw new Error('#DIV/0!');
+400|          left = lNum / rNum;
+401|        }
+402|      }
+403|
+404|      return left;
+405|    }
+406|
+407|    // Exponentiation: ^
+408|    parseExponentiation() {
+409|      let left = this.parseUnary();
+410|
+411|      while (this.peek() && this.peek().type === TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR && 
this.peek().value === '^') {
+412|        this.consume();
+413|        const right = this.parseUnary();
+414|        left = Math.pow(this.toNumber(left), this.toNumber(right));
+415|      }
+416|
+417|      return left;
+418|    }
+419|
+420|    // Unary: +, -
+421|    parseUnary() {
+422|      if (this.peek() && this.peek().type === TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR &&
+423|        (this.peek().value === '+' || this.peek().value === '-')) {
+424|        const op = this.consume().value;
+425|        const operand = this.parseUnary();
+426|        const num = this.toNumber(operand);
+427|        return op === '-' ? -num : num;
+428|      }
+429|
+430|      return this.parsePrimary();
+431|    }
+432|
+433|    // Primary: Number, String, CellRef, FunctionCall, Range (in function),
(Expr)
+434|    parsePrimary() {
+435|      const token = this.peek();
+436|      if (!token) throw new Error('#SYNTAX!');
+437|
+438|      if (token.type === TOKEN_TYPES.NUMBER) {
+439|        this.consume();
+440|        // Check for percent postfix
+441|        if (this.peek() && this.peek().type === TOKEN_TYPES.OPERATOR && 
this.peek().value === '%') {
+442|          this.consume();
+443|          return token.value / 100;
+444|        }
+445|        return token.value;
+446|      }
+447|
+448|      if (token.type === TOKEN_TYPES.STRING) {
+449|        this.consume();
+450|        return token.value;
+451|      }
+452|
+453|      if (token.type === TOKEN_TYPES.CELL_REF) {
+454|        this.consume();
+455|        const cellVal = this.cellLookupFn(token.value);
+456|        if (typeof cellVal === 'string' && cellVal.startsWith('#')) {
+457|          throw new Error(cellVal);
+458|        }
+459|        return cellVal;
+460|      }
+461|
+462|      if (token.type === TOKEN_TYPES.IDENTIFIER) {
+463|        return this.parseFunctionCall();
+464|      }
+465|
+466|      if (token.type === TOKEN_TYPES.LPAREN) {
+467|        this.consume(TOKEN_TYPES.LPAREN);
+468|        const expr = this.parseExpression();
+469|        this.consume(TOKEN_TYPES.RPAREN);
+470|        return expr;
+471|      }
+472|
+473|      throw new Error('#SYNTAX!');
+474|    }
+475|
+476|    parseFunctionCall() {
+477|      const fnNameToken = this.consume(TOKEN_TYPES.IDENTIFIER);
+478|      const fnName = fnNameToken.value.toUpperCase();
+479|
+480|      this.consume(TOKEN_TYPES.LPAREN);
+481|      const rawArgs = [];
+482|
+483|      if (this.peek() && this.peek().type !== TOKEN_TYPES.RPAREN) {
+484|        while (true) {
+485|          // Check if argument is a RANGE_REF e.g. A1:B10
+486|          if (this.peek() && this.peek().type === TOKEN_TYPES.RANGE_REF) {
+487|            const rangeToken = this.consume(TOKEN_TYPES.RANGE_REF);
+488|            const [r1, r2] = rangeToken.value.split(':');
+489|            const cells = expandRange(r1, r2);
+490|            for (const cellRef of cells) {
+491|              const val = this.cellLookupFn(cellRef);
+492|              rawArgs.push(val);
+493|            }
+494|          } else {
+495|            // Expression argument
+496|            const val = this.parseExpression();
+497|            rawArgs.push(val);
+498|          }
+499|
+500|          if (this.peek() && this.peek().type === TOKEN_TYPES.COMMA) {
+501|            this.consume(TOKEN_TYPES.COMMA);
+502|          } else {
+503|            break;
+504|          }
+505|        }
+506|      }
+507|
+508|      this.consume(TOKEN_TYPES.RPAREN);
+509|      return this.evaluateFunction(fnName, rawArgs);
+510|    }
+511|
+512|    evaluateFunction(fnName, args) {
+513|      // Flatten any nested arrays
+514|      const flatArgs = [];
+515|      const flatten = (items) => {
+516|        for (const item of items) {
+517|          if (Array.isArray(item)) flatten(item);
+518|          else flatArgs.push(item);
+519|        }
+520|      };
+521|      flatten(args);
+522|
+523|      // Check for error propagation in arguments
+524|      for (const arg of flatArgs) {
+525|        if (typeof arg === 'string' && arg.startsWith('#')) {
+526|          throw new Error(arg);
+527|        }
+528|      }
+529|
+530|      // Filter numeric values for statistical functions
+531|      const numbers = [];
+532|      for (const val of flatArgs) {
+533|        if (val === null || val === undefined || val === '') continue;
+534|        if (typeof val === 'number') {
+535|          if (!isNaN(val)) numbers.push(val);
+536|        } else if (typeof val === 'string') {
+537|          const num = Number(val);
+538|          if (!isNaN(num) && val.trim() !== '') {
+539|            numbers.push(num);
+540|          }
+541|        } else if (typeof val === 'boolean') {
+542|          numbers.push(val ? 1 : 0);
+543|        }
+544|      }
+545|
+546|      switch (fnName) {
+547|        case 'SUM': {
+548|          let sum = 0;
+549|          for (const n of numbers) sum += n;
+550|          return sum;
+551|        }
+552|        case 'AVG':
+553|        case 'AVERAGE': {
+554|          if (numbers.length === 0) throw new Error('#DIV/0!');
+555|          let sum = 0;
+556|          for (const n of numbers) sum += n;
+557|          return sum / numbers.length;
+558|        }
+559|        case 'MIN': {
+560|          if (numbers.length === 0) return 0;
+561|          return Math.min(...numbers);
+562|        }
+563|        case 'MAX': {
+564|          if (numbers.length === 0) return 0;
+565|          return Math.max(...numbers);
+566|        }
+567|        case 'COUNT': {
+568|          return numbers.length;
+569|        }
+570|        default:
+571|          throw new Error('#NAME?');
+572|      }
+573|    }
+574|
+575|    toNumber(val) {
+576|      if (typeof val === 'number') return val;
+577|      if (val === null || val === undefined || val === '') return 0;
+578|      if (typeof val === 'boolean') return val ? 1 : 0;
+579|      if (typeof val === 'string') {
+580|        if (val.startsWith('#')) throw new Error(val);
+581|        const num = Number(val);
+582|        if (isNaN(num)) throw new Error('#VALUE!');
+583|        return num;
+584|      }
+585|      return 0;
+586|    }
+587|  }
+588|
+589|  // --- Evaluation Engine & Dependency Graph ---
+590|  class WorkbookCalculator {
+591|    constructor(cells = {}) {
+592|      this.rawCells = { ...cells }; // cellRef -> string raw value
+593|      this.computedValues = {};     // cellRef -> computed result (number, 
string, or error string)
+594|      this.errors = {};             // cellRef -> error message
+595|      this.dependencies = {};       // cellRef -> [deps]
+596|      this.reverseDependencies = {};// dep -> [cells depending on it]
+597|    }
+598|
+599|    setCells(cells) {
+600|      this.rawCells = { ...cells };
+601|      this.computeAll();
+602|    }
+603|
+604|    setCell(ref, rawValue) {
+605|      const normalizedRef = ref.toUpperCase();
+606|      if (rawValue === '' || rawValue === null || rawValue === undefined) {
+607|        delete this.rawCells[normalizedRef];
+608|      } else {
+609|        this.rawCells[normalizedRef] = String(rawValue);
+610|      }
+611|      this.computeAll();
+612|    }
+613|
+614|    computeAll() {
+615|      this.computedValues = {};
+616|      this.errors = {};
+617|      this.dependencies = {};
+618|      this.reverseDependencies = {};
+619|
+620|      // 1. Build dependency graph
+621|      for (const [ref, raw] of Object.entries(this.rawCells)) {
+622|        if (typeof raw === 'string' && raw.startsWith('=')) {
+623|          const deps = extractDependencies(raw);
+624|          this.dependencies[ref] = deps;
+625|          deps.forEach(d => {
+626|            if (!this.reverseDependencies[d]) this.reverseDependencies[d] =
[];
+627|            this.reverseDependencies[d].push(ref);
+628|          });
+629|        }
+630|      }
+631|
+632|      // 2. Detect circular dependencies
+633|      const circularCells = this._detectCycles();
+634|      for (const circ of circularCells) {
+635|        this.computedValues[circ] = '#CIRCULAR!';
+636|        this.errors[circ] = 'Circular reference detected';
+637|      }
+638|
+639|      // 3. Evaluate non-circular cells in topological order
+640|      const visited = new Set(circularCells);
+641|      const evalCell = (ref) => {
+642|        if (visited.has(ref)) return;
+643|        visited.add(ref);
+644|
+645|        const raw = this.rawCells[ref];
+646|        if (raw === undefined || raw === null || raw === '') {
+647|          this.computedValues[ref] = '';
+648|          return;
+649|        }
+650|
+651|        if (typeof raw !== 'string' || !raw.startsWith('=')) {
+652|          // Plain value: number or text
+653|          const trimmed = String(raw).trim();
+654|          const num = Number(trimmed);
+655|          if (!isNaN(num) && trimmed !== '') {
+656|            this.computedValues[ref] = num;
+657|          } else {
+658|            this.computedValues[ref] = raw;
+659|          }
+660|          return;
+661|        }
+662|
+663|        // Formula cell: evaluate dependencies first
+664|        const deps = this.dependencies[ref] || [];
+665|        for (const dep of deps) {
+666|          if (!visited.has(dep) && !circularCells.has(dep)) {
+667|            evalCell(dep);
+668|          }
+669|        }
+670|
+671|        // If any direct dependency is a circular reference, propagate
+672|        for (const dep of deps) {
+673|          if (circularCells.has(dep)) {
+674|            this.computedValues[ref] = '#CIRCULAR!';
+675|            return;
+676|          }
+677|        }
+678|
+679|        // Evaluate formula
+680|        try {
+681|          const tokens = tokenize(raw);
+682|          const parser = new FormulaParser(tokens, (cellRef) => {
+683|            const norm = cellRef.toUpperCase();
+684|            if (circularCells.has(norm)) throw new Error('#CIRCULAR!');
+685|            if (this.computedValues[norm] !== undefined) {
+686|              return this.computedValues[norm];
+687|            }
+688|            if (this.rawCells[norm] !== undefined) {
+689|              evalCell(norm);
+690|              return this.computedValues[norm] !== undefined ? 
this.computedValues[norm] : 0;
+691|            }
+692|            return 0; // Empty cell defaults to 0
+693|          });
+694|
+695|          const result = parser.parse();
+696|          // Round floating point artifacts if needed (e.g. 0.1 + 0.2 -> 
0.3)
+697|          if (typeof result === 'number' && isFinite(result)) {
+698|            const rounded = Math.round(result * 1e10) / 1e10;
+699|            this.computedValues[ref] = rounded;
+700|          } else {
+701|            this.computedValues[ref] = result;
+702|          }
+703|        } catch (err) {
+704|          const errMsg = err.message.startsWith('#') ? err.message : 
'#ERROR!';
+705|          this.computedValues[ref] = errMsg;
+706|          this.errors[ref] = err.message;
+707|        }
+708|      };
+709|
+710|      for (const ref of Object.keys(this.rawCells)) {
+711|        evalCell(ref);
+712|      }
+713|
+714|      return this.computedValues;
+715|    }
+716|
+717|    _detectCycles() {
+718|      const circular = new Set();
+719|      const UNVISITED = 0, VISITING = 1, VISITED = 2;
+720|      const state = {};
+721|
+722|      const dfs = (node, path) => {
+723|        state[node] = VISITING;
+724|        path.push(node);
+725|
+726|        const neighbors = this.dependencies[node] || [];
+727|        for (const neighbor of neighbors) {
+728|          if (this.rawCells[neighbor] && typeof this.rawCells[neighbor] ===
'string' && this.rawCells[neighbor].startsWith('=')) {
+729|            if (state[neighbor] === VISITING) {
+730|              // Found cycle! Add all nodes in cycle path
+731|              const cycleStartIdx = path.indexOf(neighbor);
+732|              for (let i = cycleStartIdx; i < path.length; i++) {
+733|                circular.add(path[i]);
+734|              }
+735|            } else if (state[neighbor] !== VISITED) {
+736|              dfs(neighbor, path);
+737|            }
+738|          }
+739|        }
+740|
+741|        path.pop();
+742|        state[node] = VISITED;
+743|      };
+744|
+745|      for (const node of Object.keys(this.dependencies)) {
+746|        if (!state[node]) {
+747|          dfs(node, []);
+748|        }
+749|      }
+750|
+751|      return circular;
+752|    }
+753|
+754|    getDisplayValue(ref) {
+755|      const norm = ref.toUpperCase();
+756|      if (this.computedValues[norm] !== undefined) {
+757|        return this.computedValues[norm];
+758|      }
+759|      return this.rawCells[norm] !== undefined ? this.rawCells[norm] : '';
+760|    }
+761|
+762|    getRawValue(ref) {
+763|      const norm = ref.toUpperCase();
+764|      return this.rawCells[norm] !== undefined ? this.rawCells[norm] : '';
+765|    }
+766|  }
+767|
+768|  // Known built-in functions with descriptions for autocomplete
+769|  const SUPPORTED_FUNCTIONS = [
+770|    { name: 'SUM', signature: 'SUM(value1, [value2, ...])', description: 
'Adds all the numbers in a range of cells' },
+771|    { name: 'AVG', signature: 'AVG(value1, [value2, ...])', description: 
'Returns the average (arithmetic mean) of arguments' },
+772|    { name: 'AVERAGE', signature: 'AVERAGE(value1, [value2, ...])', 
description: 'Returns the average of arguments' },
+773|    { name: 'MIN', signature: 'MIN(value1, [value2, ...])', description: 
'Returns the smallest number in a set of values' },
+774|    { name: 'MAX', signature: 'MAX(value1, [value2, ...])', description: 
'Returns the largest number in a set of values' },
+775|    { name: 'COUNT', signature: 'COUNT(value1, [value2, ...])', 
description: 'Counts how many numbers are in the list of arguments' },
+776|  ];
+777|
+778|  return {
+779|    colLetterToIndex,
+780|    indexToColLetter,
+781|    parseCellRef,
+782|    expandRange,
+783|    shiftCellRef,
+784|    shiftFormula,
+785|    extractDependencies,

+787|    FormulaParser,
+788|    WorkbookCalculator,
+789|    SUPPORTED_FUNCTIONS
+790|  };

Agent Action ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Summary: Debug regex in shiftFormula

Reasoning:
**Debugging a JavaScript Formula Shifting Issue**

Okay, so I'm looking at this output and immediately see a problem: `=*$C3` where
I expected something else. The culprit here is the dollar sign, specifically how
JavaScript's `.replace()` function handles it within replacement strings. I've 
got to remember that `$` is a special character in JS replacement strings – it's
used for backreferences like `$1`, `$&`, and `$$`. So, when I try to replace 
something and my replacement string *contains* a `$`, JavaScript will interpret 
that and try to do a backreference, which is clearly not what I want.

The fix? Simple. When constructing the replacement string in `.replace()`, I 
need to either use a replacer function – something like `() => replacement` – 
or, if I'm using a string replacement, I have to escape the `$` by replacing it 
with `$$`. That should take care of that little gotcha.

Let's dive into the `Formula.shiftFormula` function to see where this is 
happening. Okay, I see the code using the `rangeRegex` and then applying 
`cellRegex`. It appears that the replacement callback is already in a function, 
which means the `$` should be treated literally. However, I need to check the 
exact behaviour of `cellRegex`.

Hmm, I see a bit of a problem. Now, back to that `=*$C3` result. I trace the 
logic and figure out that the cell reference `=$B$2` is the likely cause. The 
first match from `cellRegex` is supposed to be `$B$2`. The important piece is 
`shiftCellRef("$B$2", 1, 0)`.

Let's look into the `parseCellRef` function used by `shiftCellRef`:
The regex here seems to correctly parse the cell reference. In this case, I can 
see that `match[1]` correctly captures the leading `$`, `match[2]` captures the 
column, `match[3]` the row, and `match[4]` captures the row number. The function
correctly extracts the absolute and relative pieces and, so far, the function 
looks to be working as expected to return `"$B$2"`.

So, what's going wrong then? Why did the shift turn `$B$2` into `C3`? I have an 
idea. The `rangeRegex` replaced ranges. Then, the `cellRegex` does the work and 
it seems it is this `cellRegex` that is the problem: 
`/(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_:\$])(\$?[A-Za-z]+\$?[1-9][0-9]*)(?![A-Za-z0-9_:\(])/g`. The 
regex is excluding `$`!

So the prefix from the beginning does not match the `$`. The problem comes from 
this part of the regex: `[^A-Za-z0-9_:\$]`. This is excluding `$`, so the 
character `$B$2` should have not been matched! However, what if the `prefix` 
variable was equal to `$`? This should have been considered.

To get a precise picture, I need to test this hypothesis. I'm going to run 
`parseCellRef` and `shiftFormula` directly in Node.js to observe the behavior, 
focusing on how the regexes interact with the dollar signs. That should give me 
the smoking gun and allow me to correct this formula shift function.




$ node --eval "
const Formula = require('./public/js/formula');
console.log('shiftCellRef $B$2:', Formula.shiftCellRef('$B$2', 1, 0));
console.log('shiftFormula =$B$2*C2:', Formula.shiftFormula('=$B$2*C2', 1, 0));
"

Agent Action ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Summary: Test with single-quoted bash command

Reasoning:
**Debugging Bash Variable Expansion with Node.js**

Okay, so I was looking at this terminal output, and it finally clicked! I see 
what happened. I was using `node --eval "console.log('$B$2')"` and thought I was
passing the literal string `'$B$2'` to Node. But no, bash got to it first! When 
I used double quotes, bash happily went ahead and expanded `$B` and `$2` 
*before* Node even got a sniff of them. And because `$B` and `$2` weren't 
defined in that bash context, they expanded to empty strings! That's why Node 
received and logged an empty string instead of the literal. Right, that makes 
perfect sense now. Time to verify this behavior. I'll test it out by using 
single quotes, or creating an actual Node script, so bash doesn't get a chance 
to interfere.




$ node --eval '
const Formula = require("./public/js/formula");
console.log("shiftCellRef $B$2:", Formula.shiftCellRef("$B$2", 1, 0));
console.log("shiftFormula =$B$2*C2:", Formula.shiftFormula("=$B$2*C2", 1, 0));
'

Agent Action ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Summary: Update task progress

Reasoning:
**Alright, let's take stock of where we are and what's next. That `shiftFormula`
fix is in, and it's looking solid – the formula engine is behaving as expected. 
Now, time to shift gears to Task 5: the frontend grid. This is a big one, so 
let's break it down.**

First, let's make sure we hit the requirements dead-on. *That* list is a 
checklist of features, functionality, and how it should feel for the user. 
Familiar spreadsheet interactions, solid error handling, and a clear layout are 
*key*. Also, the collaborative features need to be well-considered for that 
multiplayer experience.

The core of this is the grid itself. The architecture: We're talking 100 rows, 
26 columns, a scrollable container with sticky headers. We have options on the 
DOM structure: plain HTML tables or CSS grids with `div` cells. The *most 
important* thing here is: no `<textarea>` or `<input>` in every cell and no 
`contenteditable`. The active editing mode is a single floating `<input>` 
element. Formula entry needs an autocomplete dropdown and a point-and-click 
reference picker. We want it to be *intuitive*.

The selection model needs to cover all the bases: active cell, selection range, 
and highlight visuals. And the *multiple users* aspect needs a clear visual cue 
for remote selections.

Then, there's keyboard navigation. It needs to feel natural: arrow keys, 
shift-arrows for extending, tab for moving. And all the standard copy/paste 
commands. Let's make sure we cover all of the common keystrokes!

The name box is a familiar spreadsheet UI feature – we'll allow for cell 
jumping. The fill handle will extend number patterns and adjust relative 
references. Find and replace will need a dialog, and "replace all" needs to be 
one undo step.

The "Cell History" panel will be an important debugging and auditing aid and the
"Revision History" will give a way to inspect versions.

We need a user selector and clear user presence indicators. Autosave is 
essential but we'll include feedback on save status: saved, saving, unsaved 
edits, errors.

Finally, modular code is the plan. We have the files laid out.

Now, let's systematically build these components.

Agent Action ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Summary: Create style.css for GridForge spreadsheet

Reasoning:
**My Design and UI Plan for GridForge**

Okay, let's get down to brass tacks and start designing the core UI for 
`/app/public/index.html` and `/app/public/css/style.css`, alongside the JS 
files. The most important thing here is to create a UI that's not only 
functional but also clean, modern, and, critically, fully responsive and 
accessible via keyboard. We need to meticulously implement every control 
specified in `interface.md`.

Here's the breakdown of how I see this coming together:

**Header/Top Bar:** "GridForge" will be our brand. The workbook title, 
"Northwind Operations Plan," needs to be prominent. We'll have a clear sheet 
indicator, "Plan," and a save status badge that's instantly recognizable ("✓ 
Saved (Rev 1)" or "● Unsaved edits"). The "Save" button will include a Ctrl+S 
shortcut hint. Undo and Redo buttons are non-negotiable, along with Find & 
Replace, Fill, Revision History, and Cell History controls. Then, a user 
selector with dropdown, pre-populated with users like Riley Stone, Morgan Lee, 
and Priya Shah, each with colored avatar dots. A live presence legend displaying
who's online and their associated colors is a key collaborative feature.

**Formula & Location Bar:** We need a name box to show current cell addresses 
(e.g., `A1` or `A1:B3`), enabling quick jumps via input. The `fx` formula 
icon/label will be standard. The formula bar should be a full-width input 
showing the raw formula or value of the cell, allowing direct editing.

**Main Grid Area:** The grid itself will be the heart of the app. It'll be a 
scrollable container with sticky row and column headers. The corner header 
button will select all. Column headers will go from A to Z. Row headers will go 
from 1 to 100. We'll create a custom grid table with 100 rows x 26 columns. The 
selection overlay will handle primary selection borders, a fill handle, and 
range shading. Remote presence overlays (borders and colored name tags) are 
essential for collaborative work. Formula reference highlights, with colored 
dashed borders during editing, will be crucial. We'll have a floating cell 
editor (`<input id="floating-cell-editor">`) positioned precisely over the 
active cell. A formula autocomplete popup will offer function suggestions (SUM, 
AVG, MIN, MAX, COUNT), complete with descriptions. A conflict indicator, likely 
a popover, will be needed for concurrent conflict resolution.

**Drawers/Modals:** We need robust drawers and modals to handle more complex 
features. The Revision History Drawer/Modal should list revisions with 
timestamps, authors, descriptions, diff summaries, and preview/restore buttons. 
The Cell History Popover/Modal will detail the edit history for the selected 
cell. A Find & Replace Modal should enable searching and replacing within the 
active sheet. A preview banner at the top of the grid with "Restore this 
Revision" and "Exit Preview" buttons will indicate that a user is looking at a 
past version. Finally, a toast notification container will display errors, 
warnings, and success messages.

Now, let's get started on `/app/public/css/style.css` to build this out!

Agent Action ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Summary: Create index.html for GridForge UI

Reasoning:
**Building the HTML Foundation**

Okay, time to craft `/app/public/index.html`. Let's get the core structure 
defined. I need a solid framework here, a clear representation of the GridForge 
interface. The user will need a specific set of controls and a workspace, and my
HTML has to lay the groundwork for these.

First, the header: "GridForge" as the app title, then a "Northwind Operations 
Plan" workbook title and "Plan" sheet badge, with the save indicator and button,
undo/redo, fill, find & replace, revision history, and cell history buttons, 
then the user selector. The user will need all these tools.

Next, the formula bar, including the name box, formula label and input. I also 
need to provide a preview alert banner.

Then, the main grid wrapper, this is where the table will sit, so I'll create a 
table 100 rows by 26 columns, with selection overlay and fill handle. Also, the 
floating editor, autocomplete suggestions and remote cursors container are also 
needed.

Lastly, I need to include all of the modals: Revision History, Cell History, 
Find & Replace, and Conflict Resolution. Then a toast container, because users 
need feedback.

Finally, the JavaScript files need to be in the proper order: `js/formula.js`, 
`js/clipboard.js`, `js/history.js`, `js/find_replace.js`, `js/collaboration.js`,
`js/grid.js`, and `js/app.js`.

Now, let's actually write that `/app/public/index.html` file!

Summary: Create Clipboard & Fill Engine in /app/public/js/clipboard.js

Reasoning:
**Clipboard Module Considerations**

Okay, time to build the client-side JavaScript modules. Let's start with 
`js/clipboard.js`. This is where the clipboard magic happens, and it needs to be
robust. I'm thinking:

*   **Copy/Cut Functionality:** First, we need to handle copying and cutting the
selected range to the system clipboard. We'll format the data as TSV 
(Tab-Separated Values) and plain text for maximum compatibility.
*   **Paste Implementation:** Pasting is where it gets interesting. We'll need 
to parse TSV or CSV data from the clipboard and intelligently paste the values 
into the grid, starting from the active cell.
*   **Fill Logic is Critical:** The fill handle logic is key for usability. 
Here's how I envision it:
    *   **Number Continuation:** We need to handle various number patterns. For 
a single number, it's simple duplication (e.g., `[5]` becomes `[5, 5, 5]`). Two 
numbers need to follow a linear arithmetic progression (like `[1, 2]` producing 
`[3, 4, 5]`, or even something like `[100, 95]` resulting in `[90, 85]`).
    *   **Formula Shifting:** For cells containing formulas, we must shift the 
references correctly during the fill operation, which means using the 
`FormulaEngine.shiftFormula(formula, deltaRow, deltaCol)` functionality, of 
course.
    *   **Text Continuation:** Text should simply be duplicated when filling.
    *   **Drag & Button Support:** The fill handle should support all 
directions: down, up, right, left, and we will need a dedicated "Fill Down" 
button for ease of use.
*   **Undo/Redo Integration:** Importantly, I need to ensure that the fill 
operation is undoable as a single action. We'll return a batch of `{ cell, 
oldVal, newVal }` changes, allowing the application to commit it as a single 
undo/redo step.

Time to write `public/js/clipboard.js`.




Action: FileEditorAction

Arguments:
  command: "create"
  path: "/app/public/js/clipboard.js"
  file_text: 
    /**
     * GridForge Clipboard & Fill Engine
     * Handles Copy, Cut, Paste (TSV/CSV) and Fill patterns (Numbers, Formulas)
     */
    
    (function (root, factory) {
      if (typeof module === 'object' && module.exports) {
        module.exports = factory(require('./formula'));
      } else {
        root.ClipboardEngine = factory(root.FormulaEngine);
      }
    })(typeof self !== 'undefined' ? self : this, function (FormulaEngine) {
    
      // Parse TSV / CSV text into 2D array
      function parseClipboardText(text) {
        if (!text) return [];
        // Normalize newlines
        const lines = text.replace(/\r\n/g, '\n').replace(/\r/g, 
'\n').split('\n');
        if (lines.length > 0 && lines[lines.length - 1] === '') {
          lines.pop(); // Remove trailing empty line
        }
    
        return lines.map(line => {
          // Check if line contains tabs
          if (line.includes('\t')) {
            return line.split('\t');
          }
          // Simple CSV parsing (handles basic commas and quotes)
          const row = [];
          let inQuotes = false;
          let curr = '';
          for (let i = 0; i < line.length; i++) {
            const char = line[i];
            if (char === '"') {
              inQuotes = !inQuotes;
            } else if (char === ',' && !inQuotes) {
              row.push(curr.trim());
              curr = '';
            } else {
              curr += char;
            }
          }
          row.push(curr.trim());
          return row;
        });
      }
    
      // Format 2D array as TSV text
      function formatTSV(gridData) {
        return gridData.map(row => row.join('\t')).join('\n');
      }
    
      /**
       * Fill Pattern Generator
       * @param {Array<string>} sourceValues - Array of raw values from source 
selection
       * @param {number} targetCount - Number of cells to fill
       * @param {number} deltaRow - Row offset per step (e.g. 1 for down, -1 for
up, 0 for right)
       * @param {number} deltaCol - Col offset per step (e.g. 1 for right, -1 
for left, 0 for down)
       */
      function generateFillValues(sourceValues, targetCount, deltaRow, deltaCol)
{
        if (sourceValues.length === 0 || targetCount <= 0) return [];
    
        // Check if source values are all formulas
        const allFormulas = sourceValues.every(v => typeof v === 'string' && 
v.startsWith('='));
        if (allFormulas) {
          const results = [];
          const srcLen = sourceValues.length;
          for (let i = 0; i < targetCount; i++) {
            const srcIdx = i % srcLen;
            const cycle = Math.floor(i / srcLen) + 1;
            const totalDeltaRow = deltaRow * (srcLen * (cycle - 1) + (srcIdx + 
1));
            const totalDeltaCol = deltaCol * (srcLen * (cycle - 1) + (srcIdx + 
1));
            const shifted = FormulaEngine.shiftFormula(sourceValues[srcIdx], 
totalDeltaRow, totalDeltaCol);
            results.push(shifted);
          }
          return results;
        }
    
        // Check if source values are numbers for arithmetic progression
        const numericValues = sourceValues.map(v => {
          const num = Number(v);
          return (!isNaN(num) && v !== '' && v !== null && v !== undefined) ? 
num : null;
        });
    
        const isAllNumeric = numericValues.every(n => n !== null);
    
        if (isAllNumeric && numericValues.length >= 2) {
          // Linear regression / step progression
          const step = (numericValues[numericValues.length - 1] - 
numericValues[0]) / (numericValues.length - 1);
          const results = [];
          let lastVal = numericValues[numericValues.length - 1];
          for (let i = 0; i < targetCount; i++) {
            lastVal += step;
            // Round to avoid floating point anomalies
            const rounded = Math.round(lastVal * 1e8) / 1e8;
            results.push(String(rounded));
          }
          return results;
        } else if (isAllNumeric && numericValues.length === 1) {
          // Single number: repeat value
          return Array(targetCount).fill(String(numericValues[0]));
        }
    
        // Mixed or text values: repeating pattern
        const results = [];
        for (let i = 0; i < targetCount; i++) {
          const val = sourceValues[i % sourceValues.length];
          if (typeof val === 'string' && val.startsWith('=')) {
            const cycle = Math.floor(i / sourceValues.length) + 1;
            const shifted = FormulaEngine.shiftFormula(val, deltaRow * cycle, 
deltaCol * cycle);
            results.push(shifted);
          } else {
            results.push(val !== undefined && val !== null ? String(val) : '');
          }
        }
        return results;
      }
    
      return {
        parseClipboardText,
        formatTSV,
        generateFillValues
      };
    });
    

  kind: "FileEditorAction"

Agent Action ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Summary: Create UndoRedoStack and history helpers in /app/public/js/history.js

Reasoning:
**My Plan for the History.js Module**

Okay, so I need to get this `history.js` file rolling. Let's break down the core
functionalities. First up, the undo/redo stack. This is fundamental, and I need 
to make sure it handles a few crucial action types: specifically, `cell_edit`, 
`batch_edit` (for things like paste, fill, replace, clear), and importantly, 
`restore` operations. The data structure will maintain both `undoStack` and 
`redoStack`, which are key to intuitive user interaction, and I'll need to 
define a maximum stack size, likely around 100 entries, to prevent memory 
issues.

Next, I need to design the Revision History Manager. This part is about 
interfacing with the server. I'll need to fetch the entire revision history 
using an API endpoint, probably something like `/api/workbooks/:id/revisions`. 
Also, I'll need an endpoint to fetch a *specific* revision snapshot, something 
like `/api/workbooks/:id/revisions/:rev`. This is for previews. When a user 
wants to preview a revision, I'll load its snapshot into a preview state, 
without disturbing the active draft. If the user wants to restore, then I’ll 
need to make it undoable. Meaning I need to place the cells from the revision 
into an undoable draft, and mark the entire draft as needing to be saved.

Finally, there is the Cell History Inspector. This is all about displaying the 
granular history for a particular cell. I'll need an endpoint to get this data, 
probably using `/api/workbooks/:id/sheets/:sheetId/cells/:cellRef/history`. I'll
also need to format the data that comes back. That includes the timestamp, the 
author of the edit, the previous value, and the new value.

Alright, that's the plan. Time to start coding `public/js/history.js`.




Action: FileEditorAction

Arguments:
  command: "create"
  path: "/app/public/js/history.js"
  file_text: 
    /**
     * GridForge History & Revision Management
     * Full Undo/Redo stack, Revision Browser, Preview & Restore, Cell History 
Inspector
     */
    
    (function (root, factory) {
      if (typeof module === 'object' && module.exports) {
        module.exports = factory();
      } else {
        root.HistoryManager = factory();
      }
    })(typeof self !== 'undefined' ? self : this, function () {
    
      class UndoRedoStack {
        constructor(options = {}) {
          this.maxSize = options.maxSize || 100;
          this.undoStack = [];
          this.redoStack = [];
          this.onStateChange = options.onStateChange || (() => {});
        }
    
        push(action) {
          this.undoStack.push(action);
          if (this.undoStack.length > this.maxSize) {
            this.undoStack.shift();
          }
          this.redoStack = []; // Clear redo stack on new action
          this.onStateChange(this.canUndo(), this.canRedo());
        }
    
        canUndo() {
          return this.undoStack.length > 0;
        }
    
        canRedo() {
          return this.redoStack.length > 0;
        }
    
        undo() {
          if (!this.canUndo()) return null;
          const action = this.undoStack.pop();
          this.redoStack.push(action);
          this.onStateChange(this.canUndo(), this.canRedo());
          return action;
        }
    
        redo() {
          if (!this.canRedo()) return null;
          const action = this.redoStack.pop();
          this.undoStack.push(action);
          this.onStateChange(this.canUndo(), this.canRedo());
          return action;
        }
    
        clear() {
          this.undoStack = [];
          this.redoStack = [];
          this.onStateChange(false, false);
        }
      }
    
      function formatRelativeTime(isoString) {
        if (!isoString) return '';
        try {
          const date = new Date(isoString);
          const now = new Date();
          const diffSecs = Math.floor((now - date) / 1000);
          if (diffSecs < 5) return 'just now';
          if (diffSecs < 60) return `${diffSecs}s ago`;
          const diffMins = Math.floor(diffSecs / 60);
          if (diffMins < 60) return `${diffMins}m ago`;
          const diffHours = Math.floor(diffMins / 60);
          if (diffHours < 24) return `${diffHours}h ago`;
          return date.toLocaleDateString() + ' ' + date.toLocaleTimeString([], {
hour: '2-digit', minute: '2-digit' });
        } catch (e) {
          return isoString;
        }
      }
    
      return {
        UndoRedoStack,
        formatRelativeTime
      };
    });
    

  kind: "FileEditorAction"

+83|  };

Summary: Create Find & Replace Engine in /app/public/js/find_replace.js

Reasoning:
**My Plan for Find and Replace Functionality**

Okay, so I need to build a "Find and Replace" feature, and I'm going to start by
creating the `public/js/find_replace.js` file. The core functionality is pretty 
straightforward: I need to allow users to find and navigate matches within the 
spreadsheet cells. Crucially, this needs to work whether they're searching 
within the *displayed value* of a cell or the *raw formula* itself. I'll need 
some options, namely case sensitivity and the ability to search for the "entire 
cell only". 

For the "Replace" action, it should replace the currently selected match. The 
"Replace All" function is where things get interesting. I'll implement it to 
replace every instance, returning a structure like `{ changes: [{ cell, oldVal, 
newVal }] }`. This `changes` array is important because I need to package all of
those replacements as *a single undo step*. This will be key to user experience.
Time to get coding and write `public/js/find_replace.js`.




Action: FileEditorAction

Arguments:
  command: "create"
  path: "/app/public/js/find_replace.js"
  file_text: 
    /**
     * GridForge Find & Replace Engine
     */
    
    (function (root, factory) {
      if (typeof module === 'object' && module.exports) {
        module.exports = factory();
      } else {
        root.FindReplaceEngine = factory();
      }
    })(typeof self !== 'undefined' ? self : this, function () {
    
      class FindReplace {
        constructor(calculator) {
          this.calculator = calculator;
          this.matches = [];
          this.currentIndex = -1;
        }
    
        findAll(query, options = {}) {
          this.matches = [];
          this.currentIndex = -1;
    
          if (!query || query.trim() === '') {
            return this.matches;
          }
    
          const matchCase = !!options.matchCase;
          const entireCell = !!options.entireCell;
          const searchQuery = matchCase ? query : query.toLowerCase();
    
          const rawCells = this.calculator.rawCells;
          // Sort cell keys logically (Row-by-row: A1, B1, C1... A2, B2, C2...)
          const cellKeys = Object.keys(rawCells).sort((a, b) => {
            const pA = a.match(/^([A-Z]+)([0-9]+)$/);
            const pB = b.match(/^([A-Z]+)([0-9]+)$/);
            if (pA && pB) {
              const rA = parseInt(pA[2], 10), rB = parseInt(pB[2], 10);
              if (rA !== rB) return rA - rB;
              return pA[1].localeCompare(pB[1]);
            }
            return a.localeCompare(b);
          });
    
          for (const ref of cellKeys) {
            const raw = String(rawCells[ref] || '');
            const display = String(this.calculator.getDisplayValue(ref) || '');
    
            const targetRaw = matchCase ? raw : raw.toLowerCase();
            const targetDisplay = matchCase ? display : display.toLowerCase();
    
            let matched = false;
            if (entireCell) {
              if (targetRaw === searchQuery || targetDisplay === searchQuery) {
                matched = true;
              }
            } else {
              if (targetRaw.includes(searchQuery) || 
targetDisplay.includes(searchQuery)) {
                matched = true;
              }
            }
    
            if (matched) {
              this.matches.push(ref);
            }
          }
    
          if (this.matches.length > 0) {
            this.currentIndex = 0;
          }
    
          return this.matches;
        }
    
        next() {
          if (this.matches.length === 0) return null;
          this.currentIndex = (this.currentIndex + 1) % this.matches.length;
          return this.matches[this.currentIndex];
        }
    
        prev() {
          if (this.matches.length === 0) return null;
          this.currentIndex = (this.currentIndex - 1 + this.matches.length) % 
this.matches.length;
          return this.matches[this.currentIndex];
        }
    
        current() {
          if (this.currentIndex >= 0 && this.currentIndex < this.matches.length)
{
            return this.matches[this.currentIndex];
          }
          return null;
        }
    
        replaceAll(query, replacement, options = {}) {
          if (!query) return [];
          const matchCase = !!options.matchCase;
          const entireCell = !!options.entireCell;
    
          const matches = this.findAll(query, options);
          const changes = [];
    
          for (const ref of matches) {
            const oldVal = String(this.calculator.getRawValue(ref) || '');
            let newVal = '';
    
            if (entireCell) {
              newVal = replacement;
            } else {
              const flags = matchCase ? 'g' : 'gi';
              // Escape regex special chars in query
              const escaped = query.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
              const regex = new RegExp(escaped, flags);
              newVal = oldVal.replace(regex, replacement);
            }
    
            if (oldVal !== newVal) {
              changes.push({ cell: ref, oldVal, newVal });
            }
          }
    
          return changes;
        }
      }
    
      return { FindReplace };
    });
    

  kind: "FileEditorAction"

Summary: Create CollaborationClient in /app/public/js/collaboration.js

Reasoning:
**Collaboration Implementation Plan**

Okay, time to get this collaboration feature built out in 
`public/js/collaboration.js`. First, I'll focus on session management. I need to
generate a unique `sessionId`, probably a random string with a timestamp 
appended, something like `sess-` followed by a base-36 random string and then a 
timestamp would work. I'll need to handle those inevitable WebSocket drops with 
reconnection logic, preferably with an exponential backoff strategy for retries 
– we don't want to hammer the server. I'll also need to keep track of the active
users (riley, morgan, priya, etc.) and update this list seamlessly when a user 
switches views or connects.

Presence indication is crucial, so I'll send cursor and selection range updates 
whenever a user moves or selects something. On the receiving end, I have a few 
things to consider for rendering those remote indicators. I'll need to draw 
colored selection borders on the grid to highlight what remote users are working
on. And if, and probably when, two users select the same cell, I need to 
represent that in a clear way, maybe with a multi-line outline or multiple 
badges. I'll also have to render a presence legend in the toolbar, with each 
user's name and their associated color badge.

Finally, I need to handle incoming server updates triggered by the 
`revision_saved` message. This is where conflict detection becomes vital. If the
user has unsaved local changes on a cell that was also modified in the incoming 
revision, I need to trigger conflict detection. I can then merge the 
non-conflicting cells into the local state. And, importantly, I'll need a 
callback to notify the main application of the server update, so everything 
stays synchronized. Let's get to writing this code.




Action: FileEditorAction

Arguments:
  command: "create"
  path: "/app/public/js/collaboration.js"
  file_text: 
    /**
     * GridForge Real-time Collaboration Client
     * Manages WebSocket session, presence broadcasting, remote cursors, and 
live updates.
     */
    
    (function (root, factory) {
      if (typeof module === 'object' && module.exports) {
        module.exports = factory();
      } else {
        root.CollaborationClient = factory();
      }
    })(typeof self !== 'undefined' ? self : this, function () {
    
      class CollaborationClient {
        constructor(options = {}) {
          this.workbookId = options.workbookId || 'ops-plan';
          this.userId = options.userId || 'riley';
          this.userName = options.userName || 'Riley Stone';
          this.sessionId = this._generateSessionId();
          this.userColor = '#2563eb';
          this.socket = null;
          this.connected = false;
          this.sessions = [];
          this.onPresenceUpdate = options.onPresenceUpdate || (() => {});
          this.onRevisionSaved = options.onRevisionSaved || (() => {});
          this.onError = options.onError || (() => {});
          this.reconnectTimer = null;
          this.pingTimer = null;
        }
    
        _generateSessionId() {
          return 'sess_' + Math.random().toString(36).substring(2, 9) + '_' + 
Date.now().toString(36);
        }
    
        connect() {
          if (this.socket && (this.socket.readyState === WebSocket.OPEN || 
this.socket.readyState === WebSocket.CONNECTING)) {
            return;
          }
    
          const protocol = window.location.protocol === 'https:' ? 'wss:' : 
'ws:';
          const wsUrl = `${protocol}//${window.location.host}/ws`;
    
          try {
            this.socket = new WebSocket(wsUrl);
    
            this.socket.onopen = () => {
              this.connected = true;
              this._sendJoin();
              this._startHeartbeat();
            };
    
            this.socket.onmessage = (event) => {
              try {
                const data = JSON.parse(event.data);
                this._handleMessage(data);
              } catch (err) {
                console.error('Failed to parse WS message:', err);
              }
            };
    
            this.socket.onclose = () => {
              this.connected = false;
              this._stopHeartbeat();
              this._scheduleReconnect();
            };
    
            this.socket.onerror = (err) => {
              console.warn('WS error:', err);
            };
          } catch (err) {
            console.error('Error connecting to WS:', err);
            this._scheduleReconnect();
          }
        }
    
        _scheduleReconnect() {
          if (this.reconnectTimer) clearTimeout(this.reconnectTimer);
          this.reconnectTimer = setTimeout(() => {
            this.connect();
          }, 2000);
        }
    
        _startHeartbeat() {
          if (this.pingTimer) clearInterval(this.pingTimer);
          this.pingTimer = setInterval(() => {
            if (this.connected && this.socket.readyState === WebSocket.OPEN) {
              this.socket.send(JSON.stringify({ type: 'ping', sessionId: 
this.sessionId }));
            }
          }, 15000);
        }
    
        _stopHeartbeat() {
          if (this.pingTimer) clearInterval(this.pingTimer);
        }
    
        _sendJoin() {
          if (!this.connected || this.socket.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) 
return;
          this.socket.send(JSON.stringify({
            type: 'join',
            sessionId: this.sessionId,
            userId: this.userId,
            userName: this.userName,
            workbookId: this.workbookId
          }));
        }
    
        switchUser(userId, userName) {
          this.userId = userId;
          this.userName = userName;
          if (this.connected && this.socket.readyState === WebSocket.OPEN) {
            this.socket.send(JSON.stringify({
              type: 'switch_user',
              sessionId: this.sessionId,
              userId,
              userName
            }));
          }
        }
    
        sendPresence(activeCell, selectionRange) {
          if (!this.connected || this.socket.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) 
return;
          this.socket.send(JSON.stringify({
            type: 'presence',
            sessionId: this.sessionId,
            cell: activeCell,
            range: selectionRange
          }));
        }
    
        _handleMessage(msg) {
          switch (msg.type) {
            case 'joined':
              this.userColor = msg.color;
              break;
            case 'user_switched':
              this.userColor = msg.color;
              break;
            case 'presence_update':
              this.sessions = msg.sessions || [];
              this.onPresenceUpdate(this.sessions);
              break;
            case 'revision_saved':
              this.onRevisionSaved(msg);
              break;
            case 'error':
              this.onError(msg.message);
              break;
          }
        }
      }
    
      return { CollaborationClient };
    });
    

  kind: "FileEditorAction"

reasoning 264 (total 19.03K) • ↓ output 1.54K (total 58.33K) • $ (total 0.5020)
